# Micro-benchmark results

Every case behind the [micro-benchmark summary](performance.md#micro-benchmarks):
dotnet/performance's runtime micro-benchmarks, with its `System.Numerics`
vector, `System.Runtime.Intrinsics` and `System.Memory` benchmarks, and
NetWasm's own workloads, run on NetWasm, Mono AOT and desktop .NET 11 with the
versions and settings on that page. Each time is nanoseconds per call of the
benchmark method, the fastest of two interleaved rounds. The ratios are
NetWasm's time over the other runtime's; lower is faster.
[`compiler-qualification/micro-benchmarks/`](../compiler-qualification/micro-benchmarks/README.md)
reproduces every number on this page.

| Family | Cases | NetWasm / Mono AOT | NetWasm / desktop .NET 11 JIT |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| NetWasm workloads | 5 | 0.45 | 0.68 |
| Runtime | 384 | 0.39 | 0.70 |
| Math | 96 | 0.80 | 1.31 |
| `System.Memory` spans | 370 | 0.41 | 1.59 |
| `System.Numerics` vectors | 770 | 0.27 | 2.22 |
| `System.Runtime.Intrinsics` | 588 | 0.15 | 1.46 |
| All | 2,213 | 0.28 | 1.50 |

Five cases, marked †, never use what they compute: `Benchstone.BenchF`'s
`NewtE`, `Trap` and `Simpsn` return a constant, and two `LowLevelPerf` cases
create delegates they drop. Mono AOT's LLVM removes the work and times an empty
loop of about 10 to 20 ns, so their ratios against Mono AOT run from about 3,000
to millions. NetWasm and desktop .NET do the work. They are left out of the
NetWasm / Mono AOT means; counting them, the overall ratio is 0.29. They stay in
the means against desktop .NET.

## DoomSharp

[DoomSharp](https://github.com/davidfowl/doomsharp), David Fowler's C# Doom
engine, is timed on its own: it renders the paused opening view of E1M1 120
times, from a WAD reduced to the lumps that render reads and compiled into the
program. Each sample is a fresh process that renders two batches of 120 frames;
the second batch is timed, and every batch's pixel checksum must match. The
five builds ran in rotation, seven samples each, under the same Wasmtime and on
the same host as the cases below. The engine source is DoomSharp at
[`02a5e6f`](https://github.com/davidfowl/doomsharp/tree/02a5e6f5e1d865c257a7340fd671db872dbde9e0).
Median render time of 120 frames, with the fastest and slowest sample:

| Build | Median (ms) | Fastest–slowest (ms) | NetWasm (NetWasmGC) / this |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| NetWasm 0.13.0, `O3`, NetWasmGC | 1,545 | 1,533–1,562 | 1.00 |
| NetWasm 0.13.0, `O3`, Compact | 1,712 | 1,701–1,729 | 0.90 |
| Mono AOT, .NET 11, Wasm SIMD | 2,417 | 2,408–2,454 | 0.64 |
| Desktop .NET 11 JIT, default tiering | 1,027 | 1,022–1,042 | 1.50 |
| Desktop .NET 11 NativeAOT | 1,159 | 1,158–1,178 | 1.33 |

In a browser, DoomSharp's own NetWasm build
([davidfowl/doomsharp#60](https://github.com/davidfowl/doomsharp/pull/60),
NetWasm 0.11.1 at `O3` with NetWasmGC) renders 120 frames 1.28 times faster
than its Blazor WebAssembly AOT build in headless Chromium: 1,750 ms against
2,236 ms on the same Apple M1 Max, from 0.69 MiB of Brotli-compressed files
against 3.67 MiB.
